They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. Find behavior data in this interval\n        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_start, side='right')\n        idx_end = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_end, side='left')\n\n        t_sel = beh_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n        v_sel = beh_values[idx_beg:idx_end]\n\n        if len(v_sel) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Check data availability (matching reference code)\n        if np.abs(t_start - t_sel[0]) > binsize:\n            continue\n        if np.abs(t_end - t_sel[-1]) > binsize:\n            continue\n\n        # Interpolate to uniform bins (matching reference code)\n        x_interp = np.linspace(t_start + binsize, t_end, n_bins)\n        try:\n            y_interp = interp1d(t_sel, v_sel, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')(x_interp)\n            result[trial_idx] = y_interp\n            good_mask[trial_idx] = True\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n\n    return result, good_mask\n\n\ndef discretize_to_bins(values, n_bins=N_DISCRETE_BINS):\n    \"\"\"Discretize continuous values into n_bins equal-frequency bins using quantiles.\n    Computed across all valid (non-NaN) values.\"\"\"\n    valid = values[~np.isnan(values)]\n    if len(valid) == 0:\n        return np.zeros_like(values, dtype=int), np.array([])\n\n    # Compute quantile boundaries\n    quantiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)\n    boundaries = np.percentile(valid, quantiles)\n\n    # Digitize - np.digitize returns values 0..n_bins, clip to 0..n_bins-1\n    result = np.digitize(values, boundaries[1:-1])  # 0 to n_bins-1\n    result = np.clip(result, 0, n_bins - 1)\n\n    return result.astype(int), boundaries\n\n\ndef compute_trial_in_block(prob_left):\n    \"\"\"Compute trial number within each block.\n    Block boundaries are where probabilityLeft changes.\"\"\"\n    trial_in_block = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    count = 1\n    for i in range(len(prob_left)):\n        if i > 0 and prob_left[i] != prob_left[i-1]:\n            count = 1\n        trial_in_block[i] = count\n        count += 1\n    return trial_in_block\n\n\ndef process_session(session_info, br, show_processing=False):\n    \"\"\"Process a single session and return data dict.\"\"\"\n    eid = session_info['eid']\n    subject = session_info['subject']\n    lab = session_info['lab']\n    date = session_info['date']\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    # \"content\": \"1\\u2192From \\\"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\\\"\\n     2\\u2192\\n     3\\u2192Our goal is to decode behavior y \\\\in R^P from spike-sorted, temporally binned spike counts. For each trial k, neural activity has size N x T, where N is the number of neurons and T the number of time steps. Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps. For each trial, we constructed X \\\\in R^{N x T}  by aggregating spike counts and used it to compute a decoder estimate of the true behavior y \\\\in R^P. y is constant within a trial (\\u201cper-trial decoding\\u201d); when P = T, y varies across time (\\u201cper-timestep decoding\\u201d).\\n     4\\u2192\\n     5\\u2192We apply our models to 433 IBL sessions,17 covering 270 brain regions and four behavioral variables: choice, prior, wheel speed, and whisker motion energy.\\n     6\\u2192\\n     7\\u2192 In the IBL experiments, mice rotate a wheel to indicate the location of a visual stimulus, defined as their choice (Figure 1A). For the first 90 trials, the stimulus appears randomly on either side with equal probability; in subsequent trials, it appears predominantly on one side in blocks. Mice adapt their behavior to these changing probabilities, allowing estimation of each mouse\\u2019s trial-by-trial prior belief (prior) about stimulus location. Additionally, wheel speed and whisker motion energy near the whisker pad are recorded. Whisker motion energy is computed as the mean absolute difference between adjacent video frames within a bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye. Choice and prior are static within a trial, while wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz. See STAR Methods sections \\u201cdata processing\\u201d and \\u201chyperparameter selection\\u201d for details.\\n     8\\u2192\\n     9\\u2192Data processing\\n    10\\u2192\\n    11\\u2192IBL trial-aligned dataset\\n    12\\u2192\\n    13\\u2192For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset. For prior, we also align trials to the stimulus onset, including neural activity from 0.6 s to 0.1 s pre-onset. The prior represents the mice\\u2019s estimate of the stimulus side probability. We use the same decoding window as in the previous study,27 focusing on the period with minimal wheel movements. Within each trial, we segment neural activity into 50-ms non-overlapping time bins. For each time bin, we bin spike counts using all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5,77 from each session. For dynamic behaviors (wheel speed and whisker motion energy), we select the first movement onset as the alignment event, and decode the behavior starting at the alignment event and ending at 1 s after the alignment event. The neural activity within each trial is binned into non-overlapping 20 ms bins. For each time bin, we similarly bin spike counts using all neurons from each session.\\n    14\\u2192\\n    15\\u2192IBL trial-unaligned dataset\\n    16\\u2192\\n    17\\u2192We focus only on wheel speed and whisker motion energy, as variables like choice and prior are only defined during experimental trials, which are periods when stimuli are presented and mice respond for rewards. Unlike the trial-aligned dataset, we do not align neural activity and behavior to a specific event onset. Instead, we segment the continuous Neuropixels recordings into 2-s snippets to construct the trial-unaligned dataset for each session. These snippets cover intervals both during and outside experimental trials, capturing the repetitive, structured behaviors within trials as well as the more spontaneous behaviors outside of trials.\\n    18\\u2192\\n    19\\u2192From \\\"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\\\"\\n    20\\u2192\\n    21\\u2192We trained 139 mice (94 male and 45 female) on the International Brain Laboratory (IBL) decision-making task (Fig. 1a,b). On each trial, a visual stimulus appeared to the left or right on a screen, and the mouse had to move it to the centre by turning a wheel with its front paws within 60\\u2009s (Fig. 1c). After an initial 90 unbiased trials, the prior probability for the stimulus to appear on the left or right side was constant over a block of trials at a ratio of 20:80% (right block) or 80:20% (left block). Blocks lasted for between 20 and 100 trials, which were drawn from a truncated geometric distribution (empirical mean of 51 trials). Block changes were not cued. Stimulus contrast was uniformly sampled from 5 possible values (100, 25, 12.5, 6.25 and 0%). Data extractors for various data types\\n    └── [other supporting modules]\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. KEY FUNCTIONS AND THEIR SIGNATURES\\n\\n### A. DATA LOADING FROM ONE CACHE\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**1. `load_spiking_data(one, pid, compute_metrics=False, qc=None, **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Load spike trains and cluster information for a probe insertion\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance (local or remote database)\\n  - `pid`: Probe insertion UUID\\n  - `compute_metrics`: Force recompute cluster metrics (default: False)\\n  - `qc`: Quality threshold (None = all clusters, 1.0 = only good units)\\n  - `**kwargs`: Additional parameters (eid, pname for offline mode)\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `selected_spikes`: Dict with keys ['times', 'clusters', 'amps', 'depths']\\n  - `selected_clusters`: pandas DataFrame with cluster info\\n  - `sampling_freq`: Sampling frequency (typically 30 kHz)\\n- **Key Details**: Uses SpikeSortingLoader from brainbox.io.one\\n\\n**2. `merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Merge spike/cluster data from multiple probes as single recording\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `spikes_list`: List of spike dicts from each probe\\n  - `clusters_list`: List of cluster DataFrames\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `merged_spikes`: Dict with merged spike data (time-sorted)\\n  - `merged_clusters`: DataFrame with re-indexed clusters\\n- **Note**: Uses column 'uuids' for unambiguous cluster identification across probes\\n\\n**3. `load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2.0, nan_exclude='default', ...)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Load all trials and create Boolean mask for trial inclusion/exclusion\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `min_rt`, `max_rt`: Reaction time bounds (seconds)\\n  - `nan_exclude`: List of events that cannot be NaN\\n  - `min_trial_len`, `max_trial_len`: Trial duration bounds\\n  - `exclude_unbiased`: Exclude unbiased block (probabilityLeft == 0.5)\\n  - `exclude_nochoice`: Exclude trials where animal didn't respond (choice == 0)\\n  - `sess_loader`: Optional SessionLoader object\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `trials`: pandas DataFrame with all trial events\\n  - `mask`: Boolean Series (True = include trial, False = exclude)\\n- **Default NaN Exclusion Events**: `['stimOn_times', 'choice', 'feedback_times', 'probabilityLeft', 'firstMovement_times', 'feedbackType']`\\n- **Typical Trial Duration Filter**: max_trial_len=10.0 seconds\\n\\n**4. `prepare_data(one, eid, bwm_df, params, n_workers=os.cpu_count())`**\\n- **Purpose**: Main orchestration function that loads all data for a session\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `bwm_df`: Brain-Wide-Map DataFrame with session metadata\\n  - `params`: Dict with 'interval_len', 'binsize', 'single_region', 'align_time', 'time_window'\\n  - `n_workers`: Number of parallel processes\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `neural_dict`: Contains spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions\\n  - `behave_dict`: Behavioral data (wheel, motion energy, pupil, etc.)\\n  - `meta_data`: Session metadata (eid, probe_name, sampling_freq, cluster info)\\n  - `trials_data`: Contains trials_df and trials_mask\\n- **Process**:\\n  1. Gets probe IDs from eid using `one.eid2pid()`\\n  2. Loads spike sorting for each probe via `load_spiking_data()`\\n  3. Merges probes via `merge_probes()`\\n  4. Loads trials via `load_trials_and_mask()`\\n  5. Loads continuous behavioral signals via `load_anytime_behaviors()`\\n\\n---\\n\\n### B. BRAIN REGION SELECTION\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**5. `list_brain_regions(neural_dict, **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Extract unique brain regions from neural data\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `neural_dict`: Contains 'cluster_regions' from spike sorting\\n  - `**kwargs`: Must include 'single_region' (bool)\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `regions`: List of brain regions (format depends on single_region flag)\\n    - If `single_region=True`: List of individual regions as [[reg1], [reg2], ...]\\n    - If `single_region=False`: Single list of all regions [[reg1, reg2, ...]]\\n  - `beryl_reg`: Beryl-mapped region acronyms\\n- **Mapping**: Uses `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(mapping='Beryl')`\\n- **Printed Output**: \\\"Use spikes from brain regions: [list of regions]\\\"\\n\\n**6. `select_brain_regions(regressors, beryl_reg, region, **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Filter clusters by brain region\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `regressors`: (variable name, appears to be unused)\\n  - `beryl_reg`: Beryl region acronyms\\n  - `region`: List of region acronyms to include\\n  - `**kwargs`: Additional arguments\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `reg_clu_ids`: Array of cluster indices matching selected regions\\n\\n---\\n\\n### C. NEURAL DATA BINNING\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**7. `bin_spiking_data(reg_clu_ids, neural_df, intervals=None, trials_df=None, n_workers=os.cpu_count(), **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Bin spike data into time windows, typically trial-aligned\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `reg_clu_ids`: Array of cluster IDs to include\\n  - `neural_df`: Dict with spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions, cluster_qc, cluster_df\\n  - `intervals`: Time intervals (alternative to trials_df)\\n  - `trials_df`: pandas DataFrame with trial events\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n  - **kwargs must include**:\\n    - `align_time`: Event to align to (e.g., 'stimOn_times', 'firstMovement_times', 'feedback_times')\\n    - `time_window`: Tuple (start, end) relative to align_time (e.g., (-0.5, 1.5) = 500ms before to 1500ms after)\\n    - `binsize`: Bin width in seconds (e.g., 0.02 = 20ms bins)\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `binned_list`: List of 2D arrays, each shape (n_bins, n_clusters) for one trial\\n  - `clusters_used_in_bins`: Unique cluster IDs\\n- **Binning Strategy**:\\n  - If time_window duration == binsize: Single spike count per trial (1 bin)\\n  - If time_window duration > binsize: Multiple bins via `get_spike_data_per_interval()`\\n- **Details**:\\n  - Uses `iblutil.numerical.bincount2D()` for efficient spike binning\\n  - Handles NaN alignment times gracefully\\n  - Bins contain spike counts (not spike times)\\n\\n**8. `get_spike_data_per_interval(times, clusters, interval_begs, interval_ends, interval_len, binsize, n_workers=os.cpu_count())`**\\n- **Purpose**: Core binning function for multi-bin windows\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `times`: Spike times (seconds)\\n  - `clusters`: Cluster IDs for each spike\\n  - `interval_begs`: Start time of each interval\\n  - `interval_ends`: End time of each interval\\n  - `interval_len`: Duration of each interval\\n  - `binsize`: Width of each bin\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `binned_spikes`: 3D array (n_intervals, n_clusters, n_bins)\\n- **Implementation**: Uses multiprocessing with `bincount2D()` for efficiency\\n\\n---\\n\\n### D. BEHAVIORAL DATA LOADING AND BINNING\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**9. `load_target_behavior(one, eid, target)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Load a specific behavioral signal from ONE cache\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `target`: Behavior type (see list below)\\n- **Supported Targets**:\\n  - `'wheel-position'`, `'wheel-velocity'`, `'wheel-speed'`\\n  - `'left-whisker-motion-energy'`, `'right-whisker-motion-energy'`\\n  - `'left-pupil-diameter'`, `'right-pupil-diameter'`\\n  - `'left-camera-{left,right}-paw-speed'`, `'right-camera-{left,right}-paw-speed'`\\n  - `'left-nose-speed'`, `'right-nose-speed'`\\n- **Returns**: Dict with keys:\\n  - `'times'`: Timestamps (array)\\n  - `'values'`: Signal values (array)\\n  - `'skip'`: True if error loading data (optional)\\n- **Data Sources**:\\n  - Wheel: SessionLoader.load_wheel()\\n  - Motion energy: SessionLoader.load_motion_energy()\\n  - Pupil/DLC: one.load_object() for camera data\\n  - Speed calculations: brainbox.behavior.dlc.get_speed()\\n\\n**10. `bin_behaviors(one, eid, behaviors, intervals=None, trials_only=False, trials_df=None, mask=None, allow_nans=True, n_workers=os.cpu_count(), **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Bin behavioral signals across trials\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `behaviors`: List of behavior names to bin\\n  - `intervals`: Time intervals (alternative to trials_df)\\n  - `trials_df`: DataFrame with trial timing information\\n  - `mask`: Boolean mask to filter trials\\n  - `allow_nans`: Whether to allow NaN values in output (default: True)\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n  - **kwargs**: Same as bin_spiking_data (align_time, time_window, binsize)\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `behave_dict`: Dict mapping behavior names to binned data\\n  - `mask_dict`: Dict mapping behavior names to quality masks\\n- **Special Handling** (called before bin_spiking_data):\\n  - `'choice'`: From trials_df['choice']\\n  - `'block'`: From trials_df['probabilityLeft']\\n  - `'reward'`: From (trials_df['rewardVolume'] > 1).astype(int)\\n  - `'contrast'`: Computed from contrastLeft and contrastRight\\n  - `'whisker-motion-energy'`: Tries left first, then right\\n  - `'pupil-diameter'`: Tries lef\n\n================================================================================\n\nSTEP 23 agent\n1→import os\n     2→import sys\n     3→import argparse\n     4→from pathlib import Path\n compute_metrics=False, qc=None, **kwargs):\n    28→    \"\"\"\n    29→    Function to load the cluster information and spike trains for clusters that may or may not pass certain quality metrics.\n    30→\n    31→    Parameters\n    32→    ----------\n    33→    one: one.api.ONE\n    34→        Instance to be used to connect to local or remote database\n    35→    pid: str\n    36→        A probe insertion UUID\n    37→    compute_metrics: bool\n    38→        If True, force SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters to recompute the cluster metrics. Default is False\n    39→    qc: float\n    40→        Quality threshold to be used to select good clusters. Default is None.\n    41→        If use all available clusters, set qc to None. If use good clusters, set qc to 1.\n    42→    kwargs:\n    43→        Keyword arguments passed to SpikeSortingLoader upon initiation. Specifically, if one instance offline,\n    44→        you need to pass 'eid' and 'pname' here as they cannot be inferred from pid in offline mode.\n    45→\n    46→    Returns\n    47→    -------\n    48→    selected_spikes: dict\n    49→        Spike trains associated with clusters. Dictionary with keys ['depths', 'times', 'clusters', 'amps']\n    50→    selected_clusters: pandas.DataFrame\n    51→        Information of clusters for this pid \n    52→    sampling_freq: float\n    53→        Sampling frequency of spiking data (action potential)\n    54→    \"\"\"\n    55→    eid = kwargs.pop('eid', '')\n    56→    pname = kwargs.pop('pname', '')\n    57→    spike_loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=pname)\n    58→    sampling_freq = spike_loader.raw_electrophysiology(band=\"ap\", stream=True).fs\n    59→    \n    60→    spikes, clusters, channels = spike_loader.load_spike_sorting()\n    61→    clusters_labeled = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(\n    62→        spikes, clusters, channels, compute_metrics=compute_metrics).to_df()\n    63→    if qc is None:\n    64→        return spikes, clusters_labeled, sampling_freq\n    65→    else:\n    66→        iok = clusters_labeled['label'] >= qc\n    67→        selected_clusters = clusters_labeled[iok]\n    68→        spike_idx, ib = ismember(spikes['clusters'], selected_clusters.index)\n    69→        selected_clusters.reset_index(drop=True, inplace=True)\n    70→        selected_spikes = {k: v[spike_idx] for k, v in spikes.items()}\n    71→        selected_spikes['clusters'] = selected_clusters.index[ib].astype(np.int32)\n    72→        return selected_spikes, selected_clusters, sampling_freq\n    73→\n    74→\n    75→def merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list):\n    76→    \"\"\"\n    77→    Merge spikes and clusters information from several probes as if they were recorded from the same probe.\n    78→    This can be used to account for the fact that data from the probes recorded in the same session are not\n    79→    statistically independent as they have the same underlying behaviour.\n    80→\n    81→    NOTE: The clusters dataframe will be re-indexed to avoid duplicated indices. Accordingly, spikes['clusters']\n    82→    will be updated. To unambiguously identify clusters use the column 'uuids'\n    83→\n    84→    Parameters\n    85→    ----------\n    86→    spikes_list: list of dicts\n    87→        List of spike dictionaries as loaded by SpikeSortingLoader or brainwidemap.load_good_units\n    88→    clusters_list: list of pandas.DataFrames\n    89→        List of cluster dataframes as loaded by SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters or brainwidemap.load_good_units\n    90→\n    91→    Returns\n    92→    -------\n    93→    merged_spikes: dict\n    94→        Merged and time-sorted spikes in single dictionary, where 'clusters' is adjusted to index into merged_clusters\n    95→    merged_clusters: pandas.DataFrame\n    96→        Merged clusters in single dataframe, re-indexed to avoid duplicate indices.\n    97→        To unambiguously identify clusters use the column 'uuids'\n    98→    \"\"\"\n    99→\n   100→    assert (len(clusters_list) == len(spikes_list)), 'clusters_list and spikes_list must have the same length'\n   101→    assert all([isinstance(s, dict) for s in spikes_list]), 'spikes_list must contain only dictionaries'\n   102→    assert all([isinstance(c, pd.DataFrame) for c in clusters_list]), 'clusters_list must contain only pd.DataFrames'\n   103→\n   104→    merged_spikes = []\n   105→    merged_clusters = []\n   106→    cluster_max = 0\n   107→\n   108→    for clusters, spikes in zip(clusters_list, spikes_list):\n   109→        spikes['clusters'] += cluster_max\n   110→        cluster_max = clusters.index.max() + 1\n   111→        merged_spikes.append(spikes)\n   112→        merged_clusters.append(clusters)\n   113→        \n   114→    merged_clusters = pd.concat(merged_clusters, ignore_index=True)\n   115→    merged_spikes = {k: np.concatenate([s[k] for s in merged_spikes]) for k in merged_spikes[0].keys()}\n   116→    # Sort spikes by spike time\n   117→    sort_idx = np.argsort(merged_spikes['times'], kind='stable')\n   118→    merged_spikes = {k: v[sort_idx] for k, v in merged_spikes.items()}\n   119→\n   120→    return merged_spikes, merged_clusters\n   121→\n   122→\n   123→def load_trials_and_mask(\n   124→        one, eid, min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2., nan_exclude='default', min_trial_len=None,\n   125→        max_trial_len=None, exclude_unbiased=False, exclude_nochoice=True, sess_loader=None):\n   126→    \"\"\"\n   127→    Function to load all trials for a given session and create a mask to exclude all trials that have a reaction time\n   128→    shorter than min_rt or longer than max_rt or that have NaN for one of the specified events.\n   129→\n   130→    Parameters\n   131→    ----------\n   132→    one: one.api.ONE\n   133→        Instance to be used to connect to local or remote database\n   134→    eid: str\n   135→        A session UUID\n   136→    min_rt: float or None\n   137→        Minimum admissible reaction time in seconds for a trial to be included. If None, don't apply.\n   140→    nan_exclude: list or 'default'\n   141→        List of trial events that cannot be NaN for a trial to be included. If set to 'default' the list is\n   142→        ['stimOn_times','choice','feedback_times','probabilityLeft','firstMovement_times','feedbackType']\n   143→    min_trial_len: float or None\n   144→        Minimum admissible trial length measured by goCue_time (start) and feedback_time (end).\n   145→        Default is None.\n   146→    max_trial_len: float or Nona\n   147→        Maximum admissible trial length measured by goCue_time (start) and feedback_time (end).\n   148→        Default is None.\n   149→    exclude_unbiased: bool\n   150→        True to exclude trials that fall within the unbiased block at the beginning of session.\n   151→        Default is False.\n   152→    exclude_nochoice: bool\n   153→        True to exclude trials where the animal does not respond. Default is False.\n   154→    sess_loader: brainbox.io.one.SessionLoader or NoneType\n   155→        Optional SessionLoader object; if None, this object will be created internally\n   156→\n   157→    Returns\n   158→    -------\n   159→    trials: pandas.DataFrame\n   160→        Trials table containing all trials for this session. If complete with columns:\n   161→        ['stimOff_times','goCueTrigger_times','feedbackType','contrastLeft','contrastRight','rewardVolume',\n   162→        'goCue_times','choice','feedback_times','stimOn_times','response_times','firstMovement_times',\n   163→        'probabilityLeft', 'intervals_0', 'intervals_1']\n   164→    mask: pandas.Series\n   165→        Boolean Series to mask trials table for trials that pass specified criteria. True for all trials that should be\n   166→        included, False for all trials that should be excluded.\n   167→    \"\"\"\n   168→\n   169→    if nan_exclude == 'default':\n   170→        nan_exclude = [\n   171→            'stimOn_times',\n   172→            'choice',\n   173→            'feedback_times',\n   174→            'probabilityLeft',\n   175→            'firstMovement_times',\n   176→            'feedbackType'\n   177→        ]\n   178→\n   179→    if sess_loader is None:\n   180→        sess_loader = SessionLoader(one, eid)\n   181→\n   182→    if sess_loader.trials.empty:\n   183→        sess_loader.load_trials()\n   184→\n   185→    # Create a mask for trials to exclude\n   186→    # Remove trials that are outside the allowed reaction time range\n   187→    if min_rt is not None:\n   188→        query = f'(firstMovement_times - stimOn_times < {min_rt})'\n   189→    else:\n   190→        query = ''\n   191→    if max_rt is not None:\n   192→        query += f' | (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times > {max_rt})'\n   193→    # Remove trials that are outside the allowed trial duration range\n   194→    if min_trial_len is not None:\n   195→        query += f' | (feedback_times - goCue_times < {min_trial_len})'\n   196→    if max_trial_len is not None:\n   197→        query += f' | (feedback_times - goCue_times > {max_trial_len})'\n   198→    # Remove trials with nan in specified events\n   199→    for event in nan_exclude:\n   200→        query += f' | {event}.isnull()'\n   201→    # Remove trials in unbiased block at beginning\n mask = ~sess_loader.trials.eval(query)\n   213→\n   214→    return sess_loader.trials, mask\n   215→\n   216→\n   217→def list_brain_regions(neural_dict, **kwargs):\n   218→    brainreg = Also check base path directly\\n        for f in base_path.iterdir():\\n            if not f.is_dir() and filename_pattern in f.name:\\n                candidates.append(f)\\n\\n    if not candidates:\\n        return None\\n    # Sort by revision date (newest first), files without revision come last\\n    def sort_key(p):\\n        parts = p.parts\\n        for part in parts:\\n            if part.startswith('#') and part.endswith('#'):\\n                return part\\n        return '#0000-00-00#'\\n    candidates.sort(key=sort_key, reverse=True)\\n    return candidates[0]\\n\\n\\ndef load_trials(alf_path):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load trials table from session.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    trials_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_trials.table.pqt')\\n    if trials_file is None:\\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\"No trials table found in {alf_path}\\\")\\n    trials = pd.read_parquet(trials_file)\\n    return trials\\n\\n\\ndef create_trial_mask(trials):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Create boolean mask for trial inclusion following reference code.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    mask = pd.Series(True, index=trials.index)\\n\\n    # Optimized implementation.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_trials = len(interval_starts)\\n    binned = np.zeros((n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins), dtype=np.float32)\\n\\n    # Pre-sort spikes are already time-sorted interpolate_wheel(timestamps, position, fs=WHEEL_FS, corner_freq=WHEEL_CORNER_FREQ, order=WHEEL_FILTER_ORDER):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Interpolate wheel position and compute velocity (matching ibllib).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    t = np.arange(timestamps[0], timestamps[-1], 1.0 / fs)\\n    if len(t) > 0 and t[-1] > timestamps[-1]:\\n        t = t[:-1]\\n\\n    pos_interp = interp1d(timestamps, position, kind='linear')(t)\\n\\n    # Butterworth low-pass filter for velocity\\n    sos = signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_freq / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\\n    vel = view\\n\\n**`load_pupil(snr_thresh=5.0)`**\\n- Loads pupil diameter from left/right camera DLC data\\n- Applies SNR threshold to filter outliers\\n\\n#### Helper Functions\\n\\n**`get_spike_counts_in_bins(spike_times, spike_clusters, intervals)`** (from brainbox.population.decode)\\n- **Purpose**: Count spikes in time intervals for each neuron\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `spike_times`: 1D array of spike times (sorted)\\n  - `spike_clusters`: 1D array of cluster IDs\\n  - `intervals`: 2D array shape (n_events, 2) with [start, end] times\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `counts`: 2D array (n_neurons, n_events)\\n  - `cluster_ids`: 1D array of unique cluster IDs\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. DATA LOADING A\n\n### bin_behaviors\n_intervals, n_clusters, n_bins)\\n- **Implementation**: Uses multiprocessing with `bincount2D()` for efficiency\\n\\n---\\n\\n### D. BEHAVIORAL DATA LOADING AND BINNING\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**9. `load_target_behavior(one, eid, target)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Load a specific behavioral signal from ONE cache\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `target`: Behavior type (see list below)\\n- **Supported Targets**:\\n  - `'wheel-position'`, `'wheel-velocity'`, `'wheel-speed'`\\n  - `'left-whisker-motion-energy'`, `'right-whisker-motion-energy'`\\n  - `'left-pupil-diameter'`, `'right-pupil-diameter'`\\n  - `'left-camera-{left,right}-paw-speed'`, `'right-camera-{left,right}-paw-speed'`\\n  - `'left-nose-speed'`, `'right-nose-speed'`\\n- **Returns**: Dict with keys:\\n  - `'times'`: Timestamps (array)\\n  - `'values'`: Signal values (array)\\n  - `'skip'`: True if error loading data (optional)\\n- **Data Sources**:\\n  - Wheel: SessionLoader.load_wheel()\\n  - Motion energy: SessionLoader.load_motion_energy()\\n  - Pupil/DLC: one.load_object() for camera data\\n  - Speed calculations: brainbox.behavior.dlc.get_speed()\\n\\n**10. `bin_behaviors(one, eid, behaviors, intervals=None, trials_only=False, trials_df=None, mask=None, allow_nans=True, n_workers=os.cpu_count(), **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Bin behavioral signals across trials\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `behaviors`: List of behavior names to bin\\n  - `intervals`: Time intervals (alternative to trials_df)\\n  - `trials_df`: DataFrame with trial timing information\\n  - `mask`: Boolean mask to filter trials\\n  - `allow_nans`: Whether to allow NaN values in output (default: True)\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n  - **kwargs**: Same as bin_spiking_data (align_time, time_window, binsize)\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `behave_dict`: Dict mapping behavior names to binned data\\n  - `mask_dict`: Dict mapping behavior names to quality masks\\n- **Special Handling** (called before bin_spiking_data):\\n  - `'choice'`: From trials_df['choice']\\n  - `'block'`: From trials_df['probabilityLeft']\\n  - `'reward'`: From (trials_df['rewardVolume'] > 1).astype(int)\\n  - `'contrast'`: Computed from contrastLeft and contrastRight\\n  - `'whisker-motion-energy'`: Tries left first, then right\\n  - `'pupil-diameter'`: Tries left first, then right\\n\\n**11. `get_behavior_per_interval(target_times, target_vals, intervals=None, trials_df=None, allow_nans=False, n_workers=os.cpu_count(), **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Bin continuous behavioral signals to match trial structure\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `target_times`: Timestamps of behavior signal\\n  - `target_vals`: Signal values\\n  - `intervals`: Time intervals\\n  - `trials_df`: Trial DataFrame\\n  - `allow_nans`: Allow NaN values (default: False)\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n  - **kwargs**: align_time, time_window, binsize\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `target_times_list`: List of interpolated times per trial\\n  - `target_vals_list`: List of interpolated values per trial\\n  - `good_interval`: Boolean mask (True = usable trial)\\n  - `skip_reasons`: List of reasons why trials were skipped\\n- **Interpolation Details**:\\n  - Uses `scipy.interpolate.interp1d()` with kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate'\\n  - Interpolates to uniform sampling: `np.linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)`\\n  - Checks for data availability (start/end within binsize of trial boundaries)\\n\\n**12. `load_anytime_behaviors(one, eid, n_workers=os.cpu_count())`**\\n- **Purpose**: Load all continuous behavioral signals for a session in parallel\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n- **Returns**: Dict mapping behavior names to dicts with 'times' and 'values'\\n- **Behaviors Loaded**: wheel-velocity, wheel-speed, whisker motion energy (left/right), pupil diameter (left/right)\\n\\n---\\n\\n### E. TRIAL ALIGNMENT\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**13. `align_spike_behavior(binned_spikes, binned_behaviors, beh_names, trials_mask=None)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Ensure neural and behavioral data are aligned (same number of trials, valid samples)\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `binned_spikes`: Array from bin_spiking_data()\\n  - `binned_behaviors`: Dict of arrays from bin_behaviors()\\n  - `beh_names`: List of behavior names to check\\n  - `trials_mask`: Boolean mask from load_trials_and_mask()\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `aligned_binned_spikes`: Filtered spikes array (only valid trials)\\n  - `aligned_binned_behaviors`: Filtered behavior dicts\\n- **Filtering Logic**:\\n  1. Create target_mask = ones\n\n### load_target_behavior\nins`: Unique cluster IDs\\n- **Binning Strategy**:\\n  - If time_window duration == binsize: Single spike count per trial (1 bin)\\n  - If time_window duration > binsize: Multiple bins via `get_spike_data_per_interval()`\\n- **Details**:\\n  - Uses `iblutil.numerical.bincount2D()` for efficient spike binning\\n  - Handles NaN alignment times gracefully\\n  - Bins contain spike counts (not spike times)\\n\\n**8. `get_spike_data_per_interval(times, clusters, interval_begs, interval_ends, interval_len, binsize, n_workers=os.cpu_count())`**\\n- **Purpose**: Core binning function for multi-bin windows\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `times`: Spike times (seconds)\\n  - `clusters`: Cluster IDs for each spike\\n  - `interval_begs`: Start time of each interval\\n  - `interval_ends`: End time of each interval\\n  - `interval_len`: Duration of each interval\\n  - `binsize`: Width of each bin\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `binned_spikes`: 3D array (n_intervals, n_clusters, n_bins)\\n- **Implementation**: Uses multiprocessing with `bincount2D()` for efficiency\\n\\n---\\n\\n### D. BEHAVIORAL DATA LOADING AND BINNING\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**9. `load_target_behavior(one, eid, target)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Load a specific behavioral signal from ONE cache\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `target_times`: Timestamps of behavior signal\\n  - `target_vals`: Signal values\\n  - `intervals`: Time intervals\\n  - `trials_df`: Trial DataFrame\\n  - `allow_nans`: Allow NaN values (default: False)\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `n_workers`: \n\n### exclude_unbiased\n mode)\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `selected_spikes`: Dict with keys ['times', 'clusters', 'amps', 'depths']\\n  - `selected_clusters`: pandas DataFrame with cluster info\\n  - `sampling_freq`: Sampling frequency (typically 30 kHz)\\n- **Key Details**: Uses SpikeSortingLoader from brainbox.io.one\\n\\n**2. `merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Merge spike/cluster data from multiple probes as single recording\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `spikes_list`: List of spike dicts from each probe\\n  - `clusters_list`: List of cluster DataFrames\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `merged_spikes`: Dict with merged spike data (time-sorted)\\n  - `merged_clusters`: DataFrame with re-indexed clusters\\n- **Note**: Uses column 'uuids' for unambiguous cluster identification across probes\\n\\n**3. `load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2.0, nan_exclude='default', ...)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Load all trials and create Boolean mask for trial inclusion/exclusion\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `min_rt`, `max_rt`: Reaction time bounds (seconds)\\n  - `nan_exclude`: List of events that cannot be NaN\\n  - `min_trial_len`, `max_trial_len`: Trial duration bounds\\n  - `exclude_unbiased`: Exclude unbiased block (probabilityLeft == 0.5)\\n  - `exclude_nochoice`: Exclude trials where animal didn't respond (choice == 0)\\n  - `sess_loader`: Optional SessionLoader object\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `trials`: pandas DataFrame with all trial events\\n  - `mask`: Boolean Series (True = include trial, False = exclude)\\n- **Default NaN Exclusion Events**: `['stimOn_times', 'choice', 'feedback_times', 'probabilityLeft', 'firstMovement_times', 'feedbackType']`\\n- **Typical Trial Duration Filter**: max_trial_len=10.0 seconds\\n\\n**4. `prepare_data(one, eid, bwm_df, params, n_workers=os.cpu_count())`**\\n- **Purpose**: Main orchestration function that loads all data for a session\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `one`: ONE instance\\n  - `eid`: Session UUID\\n  - `bwm_df`: Brain-Wide-Map DataFrame with session metadata\\n  - `params`: Dict with 'interval_len', 'binsize', 'single_region', 'align_time', 'time_window'\\n  - `n_workers`: Number of parallel processes\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `neural_dict`: Contains spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions\\n  - `behave_dict`: Behavioral data (wheel, motion energy, pupil, etc.)\\n  - `meta_data`: Session metadata (eid, probe_name, sampling_freq, cluster info)\\n  - `trials_data`: Contains trials_df and trials_mask\\n- **Process**:\\n  1. Gets probe IDs from eid using `one.eid2pid()`\\n  2. Loads spike sorting for each probe via `load_spiking_data()`\\n  3. Merges probes via `merge_probes()`\\n  4. Loads trials via `load_trials_and_mask()`\\n  5. Loads continuous behavioral signals via `load_anytime_behaviors()`\\n\\n---\\n\\n### B. BRAIN REGION SELECTION\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**5. `list_brain_regions(neural_dict, **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Extract unique brain regions from neural data\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `neural_dict`: Contains 'cluster_regions' from spike sorting\\n  - `**kwargs`: Must include 'single_region' (bool)\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `regions`: List of brain regions (format depends on single_region flag)\\n    - If `single_region=True`: List of individual regions as [[reg1], [reg2], ...]\\n    - If `single_region=False`: Single list of all regions [[reg1, reg2, ...]]\\n  - `beryl_reg`: Beryl-mapped region acronyms\\n- **Mapping**: Uses `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(mapping='Beryl')`\\n- **Printed Output**: \\\"Use spikes from brain regions: [list of regions]\\\"\\n\\n**6. `select_brain_regions(regressors, beryl_reg, region, **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Filter clusters by brain region\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `regressors`: (variable name, appears to be unused)\\n  - `beryl_reg`: Beryl region acronyms\\n  - `region`: List of region acronyms to include\\n  - `**kwargs`: Additional arguments\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `reg_clu_ids`: Array of cluster indices matching selected regions\\n\\n---\\n\\n### C. NEURAL DATA BINNING\\n\\n#### File: `/app/code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`\\n\\n**7. `bin_spiking_data(reg_clu_ids, neural_df, intervals=None, trials_df=None, n_workers=os.cpu_count(), **kwargs)`**\\n- **Purpose**: Bin spike data into time windows, typically trial-aligned\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `reg_clu_ids`: Array of cluster IDs to include\\n  - `neural_df`: Dict with spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions, cluster_qc, cluster_df\\n  - `intervals`: Time intervals (alternative to trials_df)\\n  - `trials_df`: pandas DataFrame with trial events\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n  - **kwargs must include**:\\n    - `align_time`: Event to align to \n\n### wheel-speed\ne binning\\n  - Handles NaN alignment times gracefully\\n  - Bins contain spike counts (not spike times)\\n\\n**8. `get_spike_data_per_interval(times, clusters, interval_begs, interval_ends, interval_len, binsize, n_workers=os.cpu_count())`**\\n- **Purpose**: Core binning function for multi-bin windows\\n- **Parameters**:\\n  - `times`: Spike times (seconds)\\n  - `clusters`: Cluster IDs for each spike\\n  - `interval_begs`: Start time of each interval\\n  - `interval_ends`: End time of each interval\\n  - `interval_len`: Duration of each interval\\n  - `binsize`: Width of each bin\\n  - `n_workers`: Parallel processes\\n- **Returns**:\\n  - `binned_spikes`: 3D array (n_intervals, n_clusters, n_bins)\\n- **Implementation**: Uses multiprocessing with `bincount2D()` for efficiency\\n\\n---\\n\\n### D.
